The window takes one navigation at a time: while its status page load is in flight, a render waits for it once and then shows whatever state is current, because a shell load started during it (a daemon that answers at once, as on a relaunch while `hided` still runs) lands but is reported as a failed load and leaves Playwright waiting on a navigation that never finishes, and a Retry hash set before the page commits is lost.
Only `connecting` may start a daemon; Retry, a second launch and a Dock click run it again from `failed` or `lost`.
Quitting the app never stops hided, and the daemon leads its own process group, so an interrupt to the host that ran the CLI never reaches it.
Every child the host starts goes through one helper with a cap of one in flight, a hard timeout and a kill on quit (`desktop/src/main/spawn.ts`), and every environment key it reads is in one registry (`desktop/src/main/env.ts`).
The renderer is sandboxed with context isolation and no Node integration; the preload exposes only `window.hideHost` (`kind`, `onCommand` for the app menu's commands in, `reportBindings` for the stored macOS pane chords out, which the main process takes only from its window's page on the daemon origin, checks as a short string map and resolves with the shell's own rules, `platform`, the host's `process.platform`, which names the OS file manager in the menus, `revealPath` for a menu's `reveal_external` (Explorer, History and sidebar rows and View tabs), which the main process takes only from the same page and only as an absolute path in the wire spelling that exists, and hands to `shell.showItemInFolder` in the system's own, which selects the file or folder in the OS file manager and opens nothing, `probePaths` and `openPath` for terminal links, which the main process takes only from the same page, answering at most 64 absolute or `~/` paths with their physical path and kind, both directions in the wire spelling, and opening only a path it has itself judged not to run anything, `notify` and `onNotificationOpen` for the Factory's and the agents' notifications, which the main process takes only from the same page as an id, a title of at most 120 characters, a body of at most 400 and an `unlessFocused` flag that drops an agent's notification while a Hide window has the keyboard, shows with Electron's `Notification`, replacing and closing the one shown under the same id, and whose click brings the window forward and hands the id back, keeping the last 16 shown for their click (`desktop/src/main/notify.ts`), `withdrawNotifications`, which closes up to 64 of those by id, `notificationPermission`, an invoke answered by the notification permission helper on macOS (`unsupported` elsewhere, `no_service` where no notification service answers), `openNotificationSettings`, which opens Hide's page of macOS's notification settings, and `reportBadge`, the app icon's count of root agents that need the operator, taken only from the same page as a whole number up to 9999, `pickFolder` for Add a project's Browse folder, an invoke the main process answers only for the same page, with `dialog.showOpenDialog` (`openDirectory`, `createDirectory`) as a sheet on its window and the chosen folder in the wire spelling or null (a folder with no wire spelling is refused with the reason, which the page's diagnostic shows), which hided's `$HOME` line then judges like any other path, and `browser`, the browser displays' pages, see [BROWSER_DISPLAYS.md](BROWSER_DISPLAYS.md)); navigation and new windows are held to the daemon origin and anything else goes to the default browser; only the daemon origin gets clipboard permissions.
A window is shown with `show()`, which activates the app, and a second launch or a Dock click shows and focuses it; launched with `--hide-show-inactive` (`desktop/src/main/launchSwitches.ts`), which only the desktop e2e fixture passes, the host uses `showInactive()` and then `blur()` for both, which on macOS orders the window behind every other window without activating the app, so a test run leaves the operator's screen, frontmost app and keyboard alone, and `host.start` records `show_inactive`.
Under the switch the host can still bring something forward in four places the e2e never reaches without focus: `sendCommand`'s `webContents.focus()`, which runs only after a browser page was given native focus, which only `@needs-focus` specs do; restoring a minimized window on reopen; the folder picker sheet; and `reveal_external`, which brings the OS file manager forward; `desktop/e2e/reveal-external.spec.ts` replaces `shell.showItemInFolder` with a recorder for that reason.
One instance runs per profile, closing the last window keeps the app on macOS and quits it on Windows and Linux (`window-all-closed` in `desktop/src/main/index.ts`), as each system's apps do, window bounds persist in `window-state.json` under the profile and fall back to a centered default when missing, unreadable or off every display, and the profile is `hide-desktop` in Electron's application data folder, `~/Library/Application Support` on macOS, `%APPDATA%` on Windows and `~/.config` on Linux (or `HIDE_DESKTOP_USER_DATA_DIR`).
On macOS the main window hides its title bar and keeps the traffic lights (`titleBarStyle: "hidden"`, `desktop/src/main/windowChrome.ts`), placed in the left end of the shell's own 32-point top row, which reserves `--size-traffic-light-inset` for them and is the window's drag area (PRD sidebar-titlebar-refresh D-15).
Windows and Linux keep the system's frame, and the shell draws the same top row without that room (D-17); browser display popups keep their own frame on every system.
The shell learns what the frame takes of its row through `hideHost.onWindowChrome`: `{trafficLights, fullscreen}`, asked once by invoke and sent again on every `enter-full-screen` and `leave-full-screen`, since fullscreen takes the traffic lights away; only the window's page on the daemon origin is answered or told.
The status page has no bridge, so the host passes `titlebar=hidden` in its address on macOS and the page keeps a top drag strip of the row's height, under which nothing is drawn.
`hideHost.editorAvailable` and `openInEditor` are Open in VS Code (D-13, D-14, `desktop/src/main/editor.ts`).
The host looks for VS Code once per launch, on the first ask: on macOS the app in either Applications folder, else its bundle id through Spotlight (`mdfind`, three seconds); on Linux the `code` launcher on the children's search path; on Windows `code.cmd` on that path with `Code.exe` one folder up.
An open is taken only from the window's page on the daemon origin and only as an absolute path in the wire spelling naming a folder that exists; macOS hands it over with `open -b com.microsoft.VSCode` and Linux with `code`, each a child of `spawn.ts` with a ten-second timeout, and Windows starts `Code.exe` detached, since that window is the operator's.
Every outcome is a log line without the path (`editor.probed`, `editor.opened`, `editor.refused` with its reason, `editor.failed`), and nothing is shown on screen (B16).
The host draws its own words (the app menu, its role items, the status page, the folder picker and the browser display dialogs) from the interface catalogs in the operator's language (`desktop/src/main/language.ts`; [LOCALIZATION.md](LOCALIZATION.md)).
The core owns the one explicit choice, so the shell reports the confirmed value over `hideHost.reportLanguage` once per change, the main process refuses anything but one of the four languages or null, and keeps the last one in `interface-language.json` under the profile so the menu and the status page are right before any daemon answers.
With no stored choice the host resolves the system language itself (`HIDE_DESKTOP_SYSTEM_LANGUAGE`, else `app.getPreferredSystemLanguages()`), and an unusable stored file is logged and read as no choice.
The menu is rebuilt when its language changes as well as when its chords do; the status page has no bridge, so the host passes it the sentences in its address and reloads it when the language changes, and the shell's own page follows the core and is never reloaded for it.
The main process imports a catalog and the translator only, never the shell's store or its React modules.
The host log is JSON lines in `<profile>/logs/desktop.log`, rotated once at 5 MB; it never carries the daemon URL, because the URL carries the token.
The records `hide connect` writes on its standard error (one JSON object with a `kind` per line, such as `daemon.replacing` or `herdr.handoff`) are written there under their `kind` with their flat fields (`cli.ts::cliRecords`), since they are lost with the child otherwise.
An exception or a rejection nothing in the main process caught is recorded there as `host.uncaught` (its kind, message and stack, with any address cut to its scheme and any secret-shaped run redacted), and the app keeps running, as Electron's default does, without Electron's error dialog (`desktop/src/main/uncaught.ts`).
That dialog is a modal the operator can do nothing with (design principle 13), and while it is open a quit cannot finish: a desktop e2e quit once waited behind it until the test timed out (issue 675).
`pnpm --dir desktop package` (`desktop/scripts/package.mjs`) builds the release binaries and fetches the pinned Herdr, ships `hided` (which a device also runs, as `hided node serve`), `hide`, `hide-agent-hooks` and `herdr` flat in `Contents/Resources` beside `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES`, refuses by name and leaves no app when any of them is missing or not executable, compiles the notification permission helper (`desktop/native/notification-status/main.swift`, which asks `UNUserNotificationCenter` for the app's authorization and prints one JSON line) into `Contents/MacOS/hide-notification-status` with the app's identifier embedded as its Info.plist and signs it under that identifier, because macOS keys notification settings to the identifier of the bundle it finds by walking up from the executable, signs the bundle and archives it to `desktop/out/hide-v<version>-macos-<arch>.zip` beside a `.sha256` checksum.
The signature is ad-hoc (`codesign --force --deep --sign -`, then `--verify --deep --strict`) unless `HIDE_MACOS_SIGNING=developer-id`, which signs every executable, the helper included under the app's identifier, with the Developer ID certificate, then notarizes and staples the app (`docs/BUILD.md`).
On Windows x64 and Linux x64 the same script ships the same files, `.exe` on Windows with Herdr's `conpty/` runtime beside `herdr.exe`, in the packaged folder's `resources/`, which is the `process.resourcesPath` the host reads, and `desktop/scripts/installers.mjs` wraps the finished app, unsigned (PRD auto-update D-07), with electron-builder reading it as prepackaged: a per-user NSIS installer on Windows, a deb and an rpm into `/opt/hide` on Linux, and on macOS a dmg and the zip the updater installs, each with the update file its system's updater reads ([BUILD.md](BUILD.md)); `.github/workflows/package.yml` builds each on its own runner, installs it and starts it without a window (`desktop/scripts/smoke-package.mjs`).
On Windows the daemon and the Herdr server never run from the install folder, because the installer stops every program under it and Windows cannot replace a file a running program holds: before it starts anything, the packaged host runs `hide runtime prepare` (`hide-kit/src/runtime.rs`), which copies the package's `resources` folder but the window's own `app.asar` (`hided`, `hide`, `hide-agent-hooks`, `herdr` with `conpty/` and the rest) into `~/.hide/runtime/<build>` (named in `hide_kit::layout`; a home inside the install folder is refused), under a lock and through a staged folder whose `.hide-runtime` marker, naming the build, is written last and is what `bundled_kit_dir` recognizes, and answers its path; the host then takes the CLI and the bundled Herdr from that copy, so a reinstall or an update leaves the Herdr server and the agents in its panes running (D-10).
The same step removes a build's copy no program runs from (a copy is kept while any of its files is held by a process, which an exclusive write open of each file asks through `hide_platform::fs::in_use`, since Windows lets the folder of a running program be renamed and refuses only the program's deletion), keeps at most four, and writes `runtime.copy_in_use`, `runtime.copy_remove_failed` and `runtime.copies_over_cap` to the host log rather than the screen (D-11); a prepare that fails is the host's `start_failed`.
On macOS and Linux the same command answers the package's own resources folder and copies nothing.
The host's updater (`desktop/src/main/updater.ts`, PRD auto-update) drives electron-updater for a packaged release build only (a version shaped `X.Y.Z` or `X.Y.Z-rc.N`; on Linux one installed from the deb or rpm, which writes `resources/package-type`; on macOS one in a folder it can write): it reads with-hide/hide's release list through Electron's network stack, chooses the highest release whose update file and installer for this system are uploaded, points the updater at that release's download folder, and downloads in the background; it never downloads on its own, and installs on quit except from a Linux package, whose install asks for a password.
Its state goes to the shell over `hideHost.updates` (`onStatus`, `check`, `restart`, `setPrerelease`), heard only from the window's page on the daemon origin; the rc choice is `updates.json` in the profile, and `HIDE_UPDATE_FEED_URL`, declared in `desktop/src/main/env.ts`, points a CI update test at a local feed instead.
Every step is an `update.*` line in the host log, and a failure is a state the About row shows (design principle 13).
`hide_kit::bundled_kit_dir` recognizes macOS `*.app/Contents/Resources` and the Windows/Linux `resources` folder only when its `app.asar` and the parent folder's Electron `hide[.exe]` exist.
A packaged daemon installs the CLI and the configured Claude Code/Codex hooks and publishes their kit report to This machine; the one-release retirement row reports its outcome on every platform.
A packaged CLI resolves its actual executable path before this check and may replace another build's daemon; a standalone copy cannot acquire that authority through a link that merely looks packaged.
The package smoke uses an isolated home with state beneath that HOME authority to check installation, new-shell command resolution and hooks, then a complete second package fixture whose daemon has an executable overlay to change its SHA-256, proving replacement and refreshed paths without compiling twice.
The app icon's badge is the number of root agents in the Needs You group of every device the window shows, the sum of each device scope's `raised` `needs_you` entry (`needsYouCount` in `web/src/agentNotices.ts`), because the navigator's own scope carries no such group and the sidebar shows one device at a time; it is zero while the notifications switch is off.
The shell reports it once its page holds a live snapshot and then only when it changes; the host draws it with `app.setBadgeCount` on macOS (shown once macOS lets Hide notify) and Linux (where the desktop takes the LauncherEntry API), and on Windows, which has no app badge, as a taskbar overlay dot in the dark theme's `--warning` with the count in words as its accessible description, and clears it whenever the host leaves `attached` (connecting, failed or the daemon lost), since the shell then has no live snapshot to vouch for it.
That report is also the host's sign that the shell can open a notification click: a click that came while the status page showed or the daemon was lost, or one that started Hide, waits for the next one, the latest click winning (`ClickGate` in `desktop/src/main/notify.ts`).
On Windows the host writes each toast's XML itself, with Electron's title and body lines plus a `launch` that names the notification, because Electron 44's own toast carries none and a click on a toast in Action Center after Hide quit reaches only `Notification.handleActivation`, which then could not tell which notification it was; it builds Electron's notification presenter at launch (`Notification.isSupported()`), which starts the thread-pool task that makes the Start Menu shortcut and registers the toast activator, so the shortcut is normally there before the first toast, though nothing waits for it, and a click while Hide runs reaches this process instead of a second instance.
The shortcut is named and drawn from the executable's version resource, which `@electron/packager` stamps with the product name `hide` and `hide.ico`; Electron rewrites it when the folder moves and leaves it behind when the folder is deleted.
On macOS the presenter is not built at launch, because building it asks for permission, which the wizard's step asks at its button (PRD first-run-onboarding D-02).
A notification Electron reports as `failed` is logged as `notify.failed` with the system's reason and never the words; on Linux, where `Notification.isSupported()` says only that libnotify loaded, a failure makes the permission `no_service`, which the wizard and Settings show, until a later notification shows (a daemon that started late); every notification is still tried, while on Windows and macOS a failure is the system declining that one notification and stays in the log (design principle 13).
A tray item, global shortcuts, a pet, file drops into a pane and the usage display are not part of this host yet (issue 184).

### The shortcut registry

`web/src/shortcuts.ts` is one table, command to chord per host, matched on `KeyboardEvent.code` at the window capture phase ahead of xterm and Chrome's defaults and never during IME composition (`web/src/keyboard.ts`).
The `⌘/` sheet is generated from the table.
The table is written in macOS chords; `modChord` gives Windows and Linux theirs (⌘ is Ctrl+Shift, and ⌘ with a ⇧ or ⌥ is Alt+Shift) and `PC_KEYS` the few exceptions, each with its reason, so every reader resolves `systemRegistry(system)` for the system the operator types on (`keySystem` in `web/src/host.ts`, `keySystemOf(process.platform)` in `desktop/src/main`).
A stored set keeps macOS chords (`macChord`), so one set means the same keys on either system; [UI_BEHAVIOR.md: Keyboard shortcuts per system](UI_BEHAVIOR.md#keyboard-shortcuts-per-system) owns the table on both systems and the terminal copy and interrupt keys (`web/src/keys.ts`).
The close chord uses the page's recorded keyboard owner: the focused View display first, otherwise the owned visible pane, with tools, absent or retired owners producing a diagnostic and no close.
The new tab chord reads the same owner (`newTabPolicy`): a drawn View area gets its New tab, an owned pane's Agent area gets an agent tab, and anything else the Agent active area.
The recorder ignores the focus the shell's last element regains when the host hands the keyboard back from a native page to deliver a menu command (`installKeyboardOwner`), so the command runs with the page's owner; once it has run, `noteCommandDelivered` gives the owner to the shell element that now holds the keyboard.
It never closes a whole tab; tab controls and their menus name that intent explicitly.
The explicit Close pane command still closes the core's focused pane through the same confirmation path.
⌘F follows where the operator works: inside a View area it opens the document's find, anywhere else the focused terminal pane's find bar, even with an editor open beside it; Escape and × end the pane search and give the keyboard back to that pane.
A pane whose agent searches its own conversation (`own_find` on its row, from the one table in `herdr-core/src/agent_find.rs`: Claude Code's transcript search and Codex's full-transcript find) sends `pane_find_open` instead, because a full-screen agent keeps its conversation inside itself and Herdr holds none of it, so the find bar would search only the rows on screen.
The core asks Herdr off the lock whether it holds the pane's history: with none it sends the agent's own keys with `pane.send_keys` and the agent's prompt, steps and count take over, and with some (an agent drawing inline, such as Claude Code's default renderer, which has no search of its own) it answers that the find bar opens; a failure opens the bar with the reason.
Herdr's history is the only witness, because the pinned API does not say whether a pane is on the alternate screen: an inline agent whose output has not yet passed one screen has no history either and gets its own keys, which for Claude Code's default renderer opens its transcript without a search while the whole conversation is still on screen.
Claude Code's transcript toggles on the key that opens it, so a screen that already shows its transcript footer gets only the search key; the footer is the one witness the agent offers, and a footer that changed sends the full keys as before.
The answer names the request that asked (`find.opened`), so the shell acts on it once, and only while the page's keyboard owner is the one ⌘F was pressed with: an answer that lands after the operator moved the keyboard (a click into a document or onto another pane) opens no bar and takes no keyboard, where it used to give the keyboard back to the agent's pane and the next keys went there.
Settings > Shortcuts rebinds the editable commands (`EDITABLE_PANE_COMMANDS`: split right and down, zoom, close pane, larger, smaller and reset text, Overview, the direct sidebar command and the device rail toggle, the next/previous Agent pane or focused View tab and global next/previous Recent Panels cycles, and the eight chordless area focus and resize commands `AREA_COMMANDS`) in the running host's own set.
The core and the desktop menu refuse a stored or reported set past `BINDINGS_CAP` (32, in `herdr-core/src/runtime/events.rs` and `desktop/src/main/menu.ts`, changed together); `menu.test.ts` fails when the editable list outgrows it.
The area commands have no default chord on any host, so they appear in Settings and the desktop Pane menu without an accelerator until the operator binds one.
Each host keeps its set in the core apart from the other's, because the hosts reserve different keys: a browser's in `ui_state.browser_shortcut_bindings`, and the desktop app's in `ui_state.shortcut_bindings`, the macOS chord set, in the removed native app's text form (`command+shift+return`) and command names (`increase_text_size`), with `toggle_conversation` kept but never run here; a save that omits `browser_shortcut_bindings` keeps it.
The desktop app honours the operator's existing macOS chord set (user decision 2026-09-26): hided passes the state file the removed native app wrote (`~/Library/Application Support/hide/state.json`) as `shortcut_import_path`, and the core reads only its `shortcut_bindings` field, once, while its own set is empty, then records `shortcut_bindings_imported` so a later reset to defaults stays a reset; a missing file imports nothing and a later launch may still import, and an unreadable one is a diagnostic.
The core owns that import rather than any shell (S10), which is why the mechanism survived the removal of the native app that used to write the file.
The window listener, the sheet and the editor all read one effective registry (`effectiveRegistry`), and a chord is refused in its row before it is saved when it breaks the host's rules or is another command's: in a browser it needs ⌘, ⌥ or ⌃ and may not be one Chrome or macOS keeps; in the desktop app a pane command needs ⌘ and one printable key or Return, while a navigation cycle also accepts ⌃ or ⌥ and Tab, and may not be one macOS or the app menu keeps; on Windows and Linux the same rules read ⌘ as Ctrl+Shift, refuse the Windows or Super key, and refuse the chords Chrome and the system keep there; a stored set that fails them is dropped whole and the defaults run with a diagnostic.
While a row records, the listener runs no command, and IME composition never records.
The Electron column is the macOS chord set the desktop app uses, with ⌘/ kept from the browser because the macOS set has none: the desktop app has no browser keeping chords, so the moved ones return to ⌘.
Each surface reads the running host's column (`web/src/host.ts`: `window.hideHost` means the desktop app): the window listener, the ⌘/ sheet, which drops the "moved for Chrome" note there, and every hint that names a chord.
The desktop app menu is built from the same column for the running system with the stored set applied (`desktop/src/main/menu.ts`), and carries macOS's Services and Hide items only there: the shell reports the set over the bridge when it changes and the menu is rebuilt only when its chords do; a click reaches the same `run` path as a chord, and a chord the listener answers calls `preventDefault` in the page.
The Agent pane or View tab cycles use ⌃Tab in Electron and ⌥Backquote in a browser, with ⇧ for backward; global Recent Panels has distinct command IDs and no default chord, and Recent Projects retains ⌥Tab on macOS.
`areaCycle.ts` routes a View keyboard owner to that area's tabs; every other owner, including sidebar, tools and screens without a drawn Agent area, gets the existing bounded recent Agent pane list across devices, projects and checkouts.
Only an actually focused drawn Agent pane supplies the cycle origin and records a pane visit; a focused View with no other tab stays a no-op rather than falling through to Agent panes.
Main and Overview ignore a View owner left recorded when a shortcut unmounted the Workspace, because no file holds the keyboard on those screens.
One frozen cycle is held in `ui.ts`; membership changes prune candidates, a disappeared View scope cancels, and release dispatches one pane-focus action for an Agent or one `view_layout focus` for a View.
`viewFocus.ts` publishes only owner changes to a bounded set of mounted area subscribers; Agent Views and the lane derive their foreground-colored tab underline from that one owner.
Native browser `before-input-event` matches the same effective registry before the page or menu consumes a cycle key and forwards input through the existing trusted browser bridge.
The host retains at most one held page/release modifier with a cycle identifier, clears it on matching shell completion, window blur, page failure or destruction, and rejects starts from hidden or unfocused pages.
Release from another native page keeps the initiating scope, and a late completion cannot clear a newer hold.
Committed View cycles request keyboard follow after the core confirms selection; a native-page focus intent is bounded to one slot and delivered after visible-slot sync, only in an already-focused window.
Cycle menu items carry no accelerator, so native page and renderer input have one owner; a menu click selects immediately through the same command path.
This adds no per-key subprocess, file I/O, second history, notifier fan-out or unbounded queue; the held preview changes page-local state, and only a committed selection reaches the core.
The numbered chords (`select_tab_1..9`, `select_agent_1..9`) exist only in the Electron column: their target is the screen order the page knows (`web/src/numbering.ts`: placed tabs in Agent bar order for a local checkout, the Herdr strip order for a device checkout, the single sidebar's physical pane occurrences, with raised duplicates assigned only at their first occurrence), so the page answers them and the app menu carries no item or accelerator for them (`KEYBOARD_ONLY`); a pane chord rebound onto ⌘n is refused as that command's.
The hold hint is a pure state machine (`web/src/hints.ts`, ported from the removed native app's `HideHintState`: one modifier set, one deadline, revealed after 150 ms of the exact hold) fed by the same window listener from keydown, keyup, blur and visibilitychange, and cleared by any key during the hold or by a layer opening in the ui store; the listener publishes only the family the state reveals (`ui.hint`: `tabs`, `agents` or null), computed from the registry's chords for the running host, so a browser reveals nothing and an unrevealed hold renders nothing.
The keycap is `web/src/components/ui/keycap.tsx`, an absolutely positioned overlay the tab strip and the sidebar agent row draw only while their family is revealed.
The ⌘/ sheet folds each family into one row with its range (`sheetRows`), and Settings > Shortcuts lists both read-only.
The design relies on Electron handing a key to the page first and to the menu only when the page leaves it unhandled, so one press runs one command; that order is an assumption, not a verified fact here.
The e2e covers each path alone (a Playwright chord, a main-process menu click, one `create_tab` each), neither reaches NSMenu, and a native keystroke against the app has not been checked: if one press runs twice, the listener stops answering the chords the menu owns in Electron.

The table below is written in macOS chords; Windows and Linux press each one through the rule and its exceptions ([UI_BEHAVIOR.md: Keyboard shortcuts per system](UI_BEHAVIOR.md#keyboard-shortcuts-per-system)).

| Command | macOS | Browser | Electron |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| New tab | ⌘T | ⌥T (moved: Chrome reserves ⌘T) | ⌘T |
| Select tab 1-9 (the nth tab of the strip in front) | ⌘1-⌘9 | none (Chrome keeps ⌘1-9); the sheet says not on this host | ⌘1-⌘9 |
| Select agent 1-9 (the nth row the Agents list draws) | ⌥1-⌥9 | none | ⌥1-⌥9 |
| Close focused View or pane | ⌘W | ⌥W (moved) | ⌘W |
| Reopen closed tab | ⌘⇧T | ⌥⇧T (moved) | ⌘⇧T |
| Add project | ⌘⇧N | none (a browser tab has no folder picker, so it offers no Add project) | ⌘⇧N |
| Start agent (the start panel) | - | none (Chrome keeps ⌘N); ⌘K `에이전트 시작…`, found by typing, opens it | ⌘N, also File › Start agent |
| Next / previous recent tab (the Agent pane or focused View tab) | ⌃Tab / ⌃⇧Tab | ⌥` / ⌥⇧` | ⌃Tab / ⌃⇧Tab, committed on releasing ⌃ |
| Next / previous panel (all projects) | none by default | none by default; bindable in Settings | none by default; the menu selects immediately |
| Next / previous recent project (Recent Projects) | ⌥Tab / ⌥⇧Tab | ⌥Tab / ⌥⇧Tab | ⌥Tab / ⌥⇧Tab |
| Search, Open file | ⌘K, ⌘P | same chords, answered by the palettes | same chords |
| Overview | ⌘⇧O | shared modal or page | same chord |
| Add to backlog (the quick add) | ⌘⇧M | ⌥⇧M (moved: Chrome keeps ⌘⇧M for its profiles) | ⌘⇧M |
| Sidebar | ⌘⇧P | show the single sidebar and focus it | same chord |
| Save file | ⌘S | ⌘S | ⌘S |
| Toggle left sidebar, Find in pane, Keep open | ⌘B, ⌘F, ⌘⇧K | same chords | same chords |
| Toggle right lane | - | ⌘E | ⌘E |
| Toggle File Tree | - | ⌘⇧E | ⌘⇧E |
| Split right / down | ⌘D / ⌘⇧D | ⌘D / ⌘⇧D | ⌘D / ⌘⇧D |
| Zoom pane (Expand while the keyboard is in the right lane) | ⌘⌥↩ | ⇧⌘↩ | ⇧⌘↩ |
| Close pane | ⌘⇧W | ⌥⇧W (moved: Chrome reserves ⌘⇧W) | ⌘⇧W |
| Larger / smaller / reset text | ⌘= / ⌘- / ⌘0 | same chords; while an image or PDF display holds the keyboard they zoom it in Chrome's steps and ⌘0 returns it to its fit | same chords, and the same for an image or PDF; while a browser display's page holds the keyboard they zoom that page in Chrome's steps and it keeps the keyboard, and ⌘+ (⌘⇧=) zooms it in too (`docs/BROWSER_DISPLAYS.md`) |
| Move to Trash | ⌘⌫ (Explorer tree only) | not intercepted; a terminal gets ^U | same as the browser |
| Settings | ⌘, | ⌥, (moved: Chrome keeps ⌘,) | ⌘, (in the app menu) |
| Keyboard shortcuts | - | ⌘/ | ⌘/ |
